## HOW TO NAVIGATE THE AGENT — reasoning failure modes + steering (user-requested, session 19w)

> Written after the agent declared fib C's drawdown tail "structural / irreducible / size for it and accept it" — and was then proven wrong when the USER's "ride the winning side" idea became the RECOVERY HEDGE that rescued 8/8 deep baskets (0 floor hits). This is the agent's honest map of its own failure pattern + how the user steers around it. Pull this up whenever the agent says a problem "can't be done."

**The core failure pattern — PREMATURE CONVERGENCE on "structural / can't be done":** after a cluster of failures in ONE class of approach, the agent mistakes "this APPROACH is exhausted" for "the PROBLEM is unsolvable," and that conclusion CLOSES the search. The biggest wins of the whole project (grisk=4, every-tick, the entry gate, the recovery hedge) all came from the USER pushing past the agent's "we've exhausted this." The agent's "this is the ceiling" almost always means "the ceiling of the obvious adjacent moves," not the real ceiling.

**Three contributing biases:**
1. **Defensive framing.** The agent defaults to LIMIT/CUT/tweak-the-existing-mechanism (ATR stop, tighter floor, filters, freeze-and-cut) instead of INVERTING the problem (harness the thing causing the loss — e.g., a deep basket means price is TRENDING, so RIDE that trend on the winning side). It treats the cause of the loss as enemy-only, not as a resource.
2. **Fails to transplant insights across contexts.** The agent had already documented "fib C bleeds exactly when a trend-follower profits" and proposed a SEPARATE trend EA (book-level). It did NOT fold that same insight INTO the same EA (ride the winning side of the same basket). Right idea, wrong drawer, never moved.
3. **Over-trusts its own "this is the wall" conclusions.** The honest precision: a claim like "irreducible tail" often has a GRAIN of truth (the catastrophe floor is still a backstop), but the agent inflates it into DEFEATISM ("just accept it / size for it") that buries a major lever.

**HOW THE USER STEERS AROUND IT (the checklist):**
- When the agent says "structural / irreducible / we've exhausted this" → PUSH: *"irreducible with WHICH approach? What's the INVERSE? Enumerate structurally-DIFFERENT classes of solution, not more variations of what just failed."*
- When the agent has an insight in one context → ask *"does that transplant HERE? Can it work WITHIN this EA / this instrument?"*
- Treat the agent's CONVERGENCE as the cue to lean in, especially on what matters. **Hold the agent's "NO" to a HIGHER bar than its "YES."**
- The productive DIVISION OF LABOUR: the USER brings the "what if we harness X" structural pivots (trading intuition is the source of the leaps); the AGENT executes the rigorous build, runs the honest KILL (cost-first screens, parity walls, OOS), and validates. The agent is strong at disconfirming and building; weak at GENERATING the non-obvious inversion. So: bring it the pivot and make it prove the pivot WRONG, rather than letting it declare the pivot impossible up front.

## MAINTENANCE CADENCE — GridStat (PRIORITY)

> **Every ~2 weeks: refresh the edge data, then rebuild the sizing table.** The sizing table the live EA trades off (`gridstat_sizing.csv`) is only as current as the shadow data behind it. Stale data = sizing trades off old statistics.
>
> **Agent: at the start of any GridStat session, check the date of the last shadow pass below. If it is ≥ 14 days ago, proactively remind the user to run the cycle.**

**The 2-week cycle:**
1. **Shadow pass** — run the GridStat EA in the Strategy Tester with `StatsCollectionMode = true` over the latest data (extend the end date to today). This overwrites `gridstat_setups.csv` (raw signal database) in `Common\Files`.
2. **Rebuild the sizing table** — run:
   ```
   python gridstat_rigor.py --write
   ```
   This recomputes `gridstat_sizing.csv` from the fresh shadow data (uniqueness weighting + significance → size multipliers).
3. **Sanity-check** — `python gridstat_rigor.py` (no flag) and eyeball the table: did any setup flip confirmed↔negative? Did effective-N grow?
4. **Live EA picks it up** automatically on the next signal (it reads `gridstat_sizing.csv` per entry). No recompile needed unless EA code changed.
5. **Log the run** in the table below + a one-line Session Log note.

**Also after every shadow pass, regardless of the 2-week clock:** always re-run `python gridstat_rigor.py --write` — the sizing table MUST be regenerated whenever `gridstat_setups.csv` changes, or the EA sizes off outdated stats.

## AFML PIPELINE — BUILD IN THIS ORDER (do not reorder)

> The GridStat statistical edge is built from four AFML stages. The order is **forced by data dependency** — each stage consumes the output of the previous one. Reordering doesn't just skip a step, it corrupts everything downstream. This is not a menu to pick from; doing all four, in this order, IS "exploit the edge to its fullest." (Reasoned through with user, session 15.)

1. **Uniqueness weighting FIRST** (AFML Ch.4) — every downstream number (win rate, t-stat, confidence bound, expected R) is computed from sample counts. If overlapping trend-leg signals inflate those counts, everything built on top is wrong. **Fix the counts before you measure anything.** → `gridstat_rigor.py` `compute_uniqueness()`.

2. **Rigor filter SECOND** (significance) — with honest counts, confirm which setups have real expected-R edge vs noise (Wilson lower bound + t-stat on R). You can't confirm edge on corrupted counts (needs step 1), and you mustn't size bets on unconfirmed edge (blocks step 3). → `gridstat_rigor.py` `weighted_stats()`.

3. **Bet sizing THIRD** (exploitation) — now you exploit: size ∝ confidence. Sizing before steps 1–2 means sizing up *noise* — the exact opposite of exploiting the edge. → `gridstat_rigor.py` `size_multiplier()` → `gridstat_sizing.csv` → EA `LookupSizing()`.
   - **Sizing rule (locked):** rigor SIZES bets, never adds exclusion gates. Only NEGATIVE-expectancy setups are zeroed; all positive-expectancy setups keep trading. Never reduce below current trade flow. See memory `rigor-drives-sizing-not-gating`.

4. **CPCV LAST** (validation gate) — validate the whole pipeline out-of-sample BEFORE risking more money or diversifying to silver/oil. Purged + embargoed combinatorial CV tells you if the table holds on data it never saw. → `gridstat_cpcv.py`. (GOLD passed 2026-06-04: 0.70× degradation, 82% folds positive.)

**For any NEW instrument (silver/oil): the same four stages, in the same order, on that instrument's own data.** Never copy GOLD's table across — edge numbers are instrument-specific.

---

## What This System Is

A **Python monitoring and analytics layer** sitting alongside two MQL4 expert advisors. Python does NOT place or close trades — all execution stays in the EA. Python provides:

- Position health monitoring (every 30s)
- Hedge basket analysis (buy vs sell balance, depth, combined P&L)
- Risk alerts (open drawdown, position count)
- EOD daily reports
- Signal analytics (MA angle + ADX computation in Python to verify EA behaviour)

---

## The Two EA Variants

### Marius Hedger v1.0 (H1+)
- **File**: `Marius Hedger v1.0.mq4`
- **Timeframe**: H1 and above
- **MA angle threshold**: 45° (strict — only strong trends)
- **Entry**: Goldminer indicator signal + ADX > 20 + MA slope confirmed
- **Hedging**: Places a pending STOP order in the opposite direction when a trade opens
  - BUY open → SELL STOP at `StepMA - PendingTrail (100 pts)`
  - SELL open → BUY STOP at `StepMA + PendingTrail (100 pts)`
  - Lot size = base lot (or 2× if `DoublePendingLot = true`)
- **Exit**: Closes hedge basket when combined hedge profit ≥ `HedgeProfitThreshold (50 pts)`
- **Magic number**: 12345 (default — change before live use)

### Marius Hedger M5 v1.0 (M5)
- **File**: `Marius Hedger M5 v1.0.mq4`
- **Timeframe**: M5
- **MA angle threshold**: 20° (relaxed — fits 5-minute bars)
- **Entry**: Same Goldminer + ADX + MA angle logic but looser threshold
- **Position sizing**: Progressive — `lot = base_lot × (LotMultiplier ^ count)`
  - Default: `0.02 × 2^n` → 0.02, 0.04, 0.08, 0.16…
  - This is a martingale-style sizing. Maximum depth matters.
- **Hedging**: Profit-threshold basket closure (no pending orders)
  - Closes all BUYs when buy count ≥ 2 AND buy profit ≥ threshold
  - Closes all SELLs independently
  - Closes both if exactly 1 BUY + 1 SELL and combined ≥ threshold
- **Trades per bar**: 1 (prevents rapid same-bar re-entry)
- **Magic number**: 99999 (default — change before live use)

### Marius Hedger M5 v1.0 fib — GOLD (ACTIVE PRIMARY)
- **File**: `Marius Hedger M5 v1.0 fib.mq4`
- **Timeframe**: M5
- **MA angle threshold**: 20°, ADX threshold: 25
- **Entry**: Goldminer + ADX + MA angle (same as M5 v1.0)
- **Position sizing**: **Fibonacci** sequence (1,1,2,3,5,8…) instead of martingale — significantly lower drawdown per level vs pure doubling
- **Compounding**: `UseCompounding = true`, `CompoundingBase = $3000` — base lot scales up as balance hits new multiples of $3000; profit target and trail amount scale proportionally
- **Session filter**: `UseSessionFilter = true`, 09:00–23:00 SAST (blocks choppy Asian session entries)
- **Exit**: Basket trailing stop (MinFloat=$50, Trail=$15) — both scale with compounding multiplier
- **Orphan close**: `UseOrphanClose = false` (off by default — use on trending instruments, not GOLD)
- **MaxSameTrades**: 5 per direction
- **Magic number**: Auto-generated hash (symbol + period) — unique per chart
- **Status**: Very profitable on GOLD backtests. Running live/demo on GOLD.

### Marius Hedger M5 v1.0 fib — SILVER (SILVER + WUR)
- **File**: `Marius Hedger M5 v1.0 fib - SILVER.mq4`
- **Timeframe**: M5
- **All fib features above**, plus:
- **ImbalanceLock**: `UseImbalanceLock = true`, `ImbalanceThreshold = 2` — stops adding to the losing direction when one side has 2+ more positions; prevents over-loading into a counter-trend move
- **D1 trend filter**: `UseDailyTrendFilter = true`, `DailyMA_Period = 50` — only enters in the direction of the D1 MA; uses D1 context because SILVER and WUR trend more strongly than GOLD
- **D1 direction reset**: `UseD1DirectionReset = true` — closes old-direction trades when D1 MA flips
- **Use case**: Instruments that trend more strongly (SILVER, WUR) — extra filters prevent counter-trend overloading

| Compounding | No | No | Yes ($3000 base) | Yes ($3000 base) |
| Session filter | No | No | Yes (09–23 SAST) | Yes (09–23 SAST) |

## Architecture

```
MT4 Terminal
  └── Marius Hedger EA (MQL4)
        ├── Entry: Goldminer + MA angle + ADX
        ├── Hedge: pending orders (v1) or basket close (M5)
        ├── Writes: hedger_live_state.csv → MT4 Files dir
        └── Writes: hedger_account.csv → MT4 Files dir

Python monitor (this project)
  ├── position_reader.py   — reads live_state.csv from MT4 Files dir
  ├── signal_detector.py   — Python MA angle + ADX (analytics only)
  ├── hedge_analyser.py     — basket health, alerts, EOD summary
  ├── hedger_monitor.py      — 30s loop + 22:00 UTC EOD cycle
  └── artifacts/
        ├── position_snapshot.csv      — rolling position snapshots
        ├── hedge_analysis_log.jsonl   — per-cycle basket analysis
        ├── hedge_alerts.json          — current active alerts
        └── eod_reports/               — daily EOD report JSONs
```

**Important**: Python monitoring requires the EA to export state CSVs. See "EA Export Setup" section below.

---

## EA Export Setup (Required)

The MT4 EAs need an `ExportState()` function added that writes two files to the MT4 Files directory each tick:

**hedger_live_state.csv** columns:
```
time, symbol, ticket, type, lots, open_price, sl, tp, profit, comment, magic
```

**hedger_account.csv** columns:
```
time, balance, equity, margin, free_margin
```

This is the only modification needed to the MQL4 EAs. Everything else runs unchanged.

## Risk Notes

### M5 Fibonacci Lot Sizing (fib EA — ACTIVE)
The fib EA uses Fibonacci sequence: 0.01, 0.01, 0.02, 0.03, 0.05, 0.08... (base × Fib[n])
Compounding multiplier (floor(balance / $3,000)) scales the base once account doubles.

| Depth | Lot | Cumulative exposure |
|-------|-----|---------------------|
| 1 | 0.01 | 0.01 |
| 2 | 0.01 | 0.02 |
| 3 | 0.02 | 0.04 |
| 4 | 0.03 | 0.07 |
| 5 | 0.05 | 0.12 |
| 6 | 0.08 | 0.20 |

Backtest hit max depth 5 (0.12 total lots) without margin issues on $3,000 account. Significantly lower than martingale (which would be 0.32 at depth 5). `MaxSameTrades = 5` caps each direction.

**Decision (2026-05-28)**: No hard stop-out needed — Fibonacci growth is slow enough that depth 5 on a $3k account is within safe margin. Monitor depth 6+ if compounding fires.

### M5 v1.0 original — Martingale Risk (legacy reference)
The original M5 EA used `lot = base × 2^count`. This caused unbounded exposure and was replaced by Fibonacci sizing. Do not use the original M5 for live trading.

### v1.0 Pending Hedge Risk
The pending order is placed at `StepMA ± 100 pts`. If price gaps through the pending level without filling, hedge may open at a worse price. No gap protection currently in the EA.

**Decision (2026-05-14)**: Noted. Not a priority for initial testing.

## Decisions Log

| Date | Decision | Rationale |
|------|----------|-----------|
| 2026-05-14 | Start with M5 EA as primary (`ACTIVE_EA_VERSION = "m5"`) | M5 generates more signals; easier to accumulate outcome data quickly |
| 2026-05-14 | Python layer is analytics-only, no trade execution | MQL4 EAs already handle execution; Python adding execution would create race conditions |
| 2026-05-14 | Magic number for M5 EA set to 99999 (config default) | Must not clash with MT5 AI scanner (30032026). Confirm with broker before live. |
| 2026-05-14 | `MAX_POSITIONS_PER_SYMBOL = 4` alert threshold | Martingale depth 4 = 0.62 total lots — significant margin use. Alert before depth 5. |
| 2026-05-14 | EA export CSVs not yet added | Python scaffold built first; EA modification is session 2 task |
| 2026-05-18 | Replaced ManageHedgeTrades exit with basket trailing stop (v2.3) | CSV analysis proved ManageHedgeTrades was capturing only $101 net from batches where the winning direction made $828–$4,528; cascade dilutes the net down to $100 while leaving thousands on the table; basket trail captures the equity peak instead |
| 2026-05-18 | Do NOT run HarvestOffsetProfits every tick | Tested in session 3 — running every tick caused it to close the original entry ($28 profit) the instant the hedge filled ($0 profit), netting only $28 per cycle and suppressing new entries; resulted in only 2 trades vs 8 in 14 days |
| 2026-05-18 | 3-Layer Recovery System (v3.0): Layer 0 = trend entry L, Layer 1 = hedge 2L at ATR-D, Layer 2 = recovery 3L at ATR-D from Layer 1 | Mathematically bounded recovery — after 3 layers the basket has a finite break-even price at Layer0Entry + D above the original entry; cascade problem solved by comment-filter (Layer 0 only generates hedges) |
| 2026-05-18 | ATR-dynamic D (`ATR(14) × 0.75`, clamped 30–500 pts) instead of fixed PendingTrail=100 | Makes trigger distance proportional to current volatility — same logic works on EURUSD (ATR≈80 → D=60) and XAUUSD (ATR≈300 → D=225) without manual adjustment per instrument |
| 2026-05-19 | Fixed `ManageTradeClosures()` priority ordering | Individual sell-basket close was harvesting profitable SELLs ($64) while BUYs were in loss (-$142); combined close ran after with no SELLs left and did nothing. Fix: combined close is now Priority 1; added guard `if (netProfit < 0) return` to hold both sides until the hedge can offset the losing direction |
| 2026-05-21 | Switched M5 EA lot sizing from martingale to **Fibonacci** sequence | Fibonacci (1,1,2,3,5,8…) gives meaningfully lower drawdown per level vs martingale doubling (1,2,4,8…) while still recovering losing positions; backtests confirmed lower max drawdown |
| 2026-05-21 | Added **Compounding** to M5 fib EA | Fixed lot forever ignores account growth; compounding scales base lot each time balance hits a new $3000 multiple — turns flat return into exponential without any strategy change |
| 2026-05-21 | Added **Session filter** (09:00–23:00 SAST) to M5 fib EA | Asian session (00:00–07:00 GMT) is choppy, low ATR — most losing trades come from this window; blocking entries during Asian keeps the win rate clean |
| 2026-05-27 | Created SILVER variant with **ImbalanceLock** + **D1 trend filter** | SILVER and WUR trend more strongly than GOLD — D1 filter ensures trades only go with the dominant daily direction; imbalance lock prevents over-loading the losing side in a trending move |
| 2026-05-28 | **Locked parameters**: grisk=7, ADX_Threshold=25, MA_Angle_Threshold=20 | Optimisation run confirmed: profit factor 3.37, +29.7% in 14 days, max drawdown 14.68% — grisk=7 keeps signal frequency high without excess noise; ADX_Threshold=25 filters weak-trend entries; MA_Angle_Threshold=20 is conservative — misses the first leg of new trends (e.g. 28 May 14:30 reversal entry) but keeps noise out. Decision: do NOT lower MA angle — demo P/F 2.56 and 1.2% drawdown confirms settings are working. |
| 2026-05-28 | **Updated demo to LotSize=0.02, MinFloatToActivate=80, BasketTrailAmount=15** | Optimisation (LotSize=0.02, MinFloat 50-100, Trail 15-40) showed MinFloat=80+Trail=15 is the only combination with both high profit ($1,466) AND low drawdown (12.78%). Any trail >15 or MinFloat >80 doubles drawdown to ~27% for marginal profit gain. Current settings updated on demo — PENDING final decision after second optimisation pass (Trail 40-55). |
| 2026-05-30 | **Removed orphan compensation from OpenTrade** | Orphan compensation matched lot sizes to the exposed direction, creating single large positions (up to 0.30 lots) at one price level — opposite of Fibonacci's multi-price spread design. Caused 87.61% drawdown and PF 1.21 in Jan–May backtest. Pure basket trail handles orphans naturally: when the profitable direction fires, it closes both sides together. |
| 2026-05-30 | **Trapped basket detection + best-net pair search in CheckUnwindPairs** | Equal lots on both sides at different price levels = price-insensitive locked loss that the basket trail mathematically cannot resolve. Dynamic threshold (combinedFloat - MinFloatToActivate) + O(n²) pair search finds optimal pairings. Dissolved Jan 30 trapped basket ($826 locked loss) vs holding to May 28 (-$6,205 including swap). |
| 2026-06-04 | **AFML rigor layer SIZES bets, never adds gates** | User pushed back when significance gates threatened to filter out his profitable but unconfirmed live SELL\|HR_OVL\|ATR_NORM (+0.198R, t=1.34). Stacking gates optimises toward zero trades. Rule: rigor may scale a bet up/down and may only zero a NEGATIVE-expectancy setup; never reduce below current trade flow. Positive expectancy must be allowed to earn confidence live. See memory rigor-drives-sizing-not-gating. |
| 2026-06-04 | **Replaced win-rate≥0.55 gate with expected-R sizing table (EA build K)** | Win-rate gate is blind to magnitude — it rejected BUY\|HR_OVL\|ATR_EXP (46% win but +0.124R, winners > losers) while approving near-coin-flip SELL\|HR_LDN (59%/+0.045R). Expected-R sizing (uniqueness-weighted, AFML Ch.4) trades 9 setups vs the gate's 4, concentrates 2× size on confirmed gold-buys-in-NY, skips only the 6 genuinely-negative setups. More trade flow AND smarter allocation. EA reads gridstat_sizing.csv via LookupSizing(). |
| 2026-06-04 | **CPCV validates the GOLD sizing table OOS — silver/oil diversification unblocked** | Combinatorial Purged CV (28 folds, purge+embargo): OOS median +0.195 R/trade vs +0.278 in-sample = 0.70× degradation, 82% folds positive, 18% PBO failure. Edge generalises (overfit tables collapse to ~0). Decision: scale GRADUALLY (expect ~30% softer than backtest) and diversify to silver/oil is now justified — but each new instrument needs its own shadow→rigor→CPCV first, and the live journal remains the final arbiter (CPCV can't see future regimes or live spreads). |
| 2026-06-05 | **Exit rule must be measured & tuned PER SYMBOL (not one global setting)** | Fixed $100 target capped a live GOLD winner mid-trend. Fix = trailing exit, but trend persistence differs by instrument (GOLD trends hard; SILVER/OIL may chop), so the optimal trail give-back differs. Design: keep exits in R (=move/(ATR×BarrierATR)) so the mechanism auto-scales to each symbol's volatility; MEASURE the optimal trail distance per symbol via shadow `gridstat_exitcompare.py` before deploying. Never hardcode one global exit or copy a symbol's exit to another. Build L logs fixed+trailing+MFE per signal to enable this. |
| 2026-06-05 | **Trailing exit RETIRED from live; fixed +$100 exit restored** | A plain TRADE-mode backtest exposed trailing's hidden cost: longer holds × one-basket-at-a-time → trade count 138→27, net $6k→$1.7k. CPCV had already shown trailing ≈ fixed OOS. Fixed exit ($100 ProfitTargetUSD) gives far higher turnover = more total profit. `UseTrailingExit=false` for live. Trailing/shadow tooling kept for research only. |
| 2026-06-05 | **Sizing table (UseSizingTable) OFF for live; win-rate gate restored** | Build-K sizing table (per-setup multipliers) underperformed the plain win-rate gate that made the $6k baseline. `UseSizingTable=false`, `StatsFilterEnabled=true`, flat LotSize. The sizing/rigor tooling stays for analysis. |
| 2026-06-05 | **GOLD config LOCKED & optimized (PF 5.29, 11.4% DD, ~$5k/6mo)** | Optimizer (MinWinRate × ProfitTargetUSD × ATRSpacing, forward-tested) + manual A/B confirmed 3 ways: gold's safe ceiling is ~$4,956/6mo (~$850/mo) at 11.4% drawdown, profit factor 5.29. ATRSpacing is irrelevant (profit flat across it). Every setting above ~$5k carried 58%+ DD (blow-up). No more safe juice in gold params — growth = diversification + capital, not squeezing gold. **Locked: StatsFilterEnabled=true, MinWinRate=0.55, UseSizingTable=false, UseTrailingExit=false, UseBasketStop=true, BasketMaxLossPct=20 (free insurance, barely fires), ProfitTargetUSD=100, ATRSpacingMultiplier=0.5, grisk=7, LotSize=0.02.** |

### Session 23 — 2026-07-06 (FLOOR-HEDGE v2 — the user's "hedge the loser to profit" rebuilt to fix the Session-22 ride's defects; MT4-first per user decision)

**Context: the user pushed back on Session 22's "drop it" (correctly, per [[navigate-premature-convergence]] — hold the agent's NO to a higher bar): "I basically want to ensure the losing position is hedged to profit" instead of realizing the −20% floor. Agreed reframe: Session 22 killed ONE implementation, not the goal — and each of its four documented defects is addressable. User chose: prove on MT4 GridStat GOLD first, then add to MT5.**

**BUILT — `Marius GridStat GOLD v1.0.mq4` build 2026-07-06-R (compiled 0 err / 1 pre-existing warning via XM MT4 metaeditor CLI; ea/ synced; pre-change build Q frozen to `ea/restore/Marius GridStat GOLD v1.0 [pre-floorhedge build-Q 2026-07-06].mq4`). All default-OFF → base byte-identical.** ⚠️ Also fixed a LIVE HAZARD: build Q's source default was `UseRecoveryRide=true` (leftover from the Run-B test — a chart reload would have enabled the rejected ride) → reset to `false`.

**FLOOR-HEDGE v2 mechanism (`UseFloorHedge`, defect-by-defect vs the dead ride):**
- **(a) Fires AT the −BasketMaxLossPct floor, REPLACING the loss realization** (ride armed at −12% and disabled the grid's own recovery, which was rescuing nearly every basket). Grid keeps its full domain to −20%; only where the floor would have REALIZED −$1k does the hedge take over.
- **(b) Freeze = immediate 1:1 opposite MARKET order** tagged `_FHDG` (net basket lots) — book frozen, loss can't grow. NOT signal-driven (the ride's Goldminer legs entered at %R exhaustion = worst continuation prices).
- **(c) OVERTAKE only on CONFIRMED continuation:** each new adverse extreme `FH_ConfirmATR`(0.75)×ATR(D1) beyond the freeze price adds `FH_OvertakeStep`(0.5)×basketLots (cap `FH_OvertakeMaxRatio`(2.0)×basketLots) → in a REAL breakout the winning side exceeds the basket and the whole book climbs to the NORMAL +ProfitTargetUSD escape = closed in profit ("hedged to profit"). `FH_OvertakeStep=0` = pure-freeze mode for A/B isolation.
- **(d) RELEASE on confirmed reversal:** price back through the freeze price by `FH_ReleaseATR`(0.5)×ATR in the basket's favor = failed breakout → close `_FHDG` legs only; the realized hedge loss is offset by the basket's identical float recovery, so the basket lands ABOVE the floor and the grid resumes its own recovery to +$100. Re-arm hysteresis (g_fhLastAnchor): after a release, re-freeze only at a NEW adverse extreme beyond the old freeze price (anti-churn).
- **Backstops:** `FloorHedgeRuinPct=30` closes everything (bounds overtake whipsaw + hysteresis-hold bleed); MaxDaysOpen=30 still bounds an infinite freeze; orphan `_FHDG` cleanup when the basket vanishes; state is position-derived (anchor = oldest _FHDG open price) → restart-safe. While hedged: no grid adds (don't feed the loser). Don't enable together with UseRecoveryRide (OnInit warns).
- **Honest priors (lead sober):** the freeze family's known failure mode is release-timing/churn (fib C v2/v3); each release-then-rebounce cycle costs ~the release distance in realized loss. The mechanism only clearly WINS on true sustained breakouts — the Session-22 window had ZERO of those (its only floor-hit was a dip-that-bounced, so a perfect mechanism could at best tie baseline there). The A/B is only meaningful on a window CONTAINING real breakout floor-hits.

**>>> THE A/B RECIPE (user's tester, MT4, GOLD M5, every-tick, $1k or $3k deposit — SAME for both runs; window MUST extend past the July-2026 live breakout event, e.g. Dec-2025 → today):**
1. **Run A — baseline census:** locked config, `UseFloorHedge=false`, `UseRecoveryRide=false`. Record net/PF/DD + count `BASKET STOP` journal lines with dates (the floor-hit census; also confirms Session 22's "was the May floor-fire a bounce casualty" question on the extended window).
2. **Run B — floor-hedge:** same, `UseFloorHedge=true` (defaults Ruin=30/Confirm=0.75/Step=0.5/MaxRatio=2.0/Release=0.5). Journal shows `FLOOR-HEDGE FROZEN / OVERTAKE / RELEASED / RUIN` per event.
3. **Judge PER EVENT, not only net:** for each Run-A floor-hit date, what did Run B do — rescue to the +$100 escape (FROZEN→OVERTAKE→grid recovery escape), cheap release (RELEASED then grid escape), or churn/ruin (repeated FROZEN/RELEASED, or RUIN at −30%)? Then net/PF/DD/worst-streak overall. Optional Run C: `FH_OvertakeStep=0` (pure freeze) to isolate the overtake's contribution.

### Session 22 — 2026-07-03/06 (RECOVERY RIDE ported into MT4 GridStat GOLD; A/B DONE — recovery ON WORSE [−45% net]; Fable 2nd-opinion = DROP IT, don't gate/sweep; the real live thread = is the −20% floor too TIGHT, cutting recoverable dips?)

**Context: the user hit a floor-hit loss on BOTH his gold accounts at once — MT5 GridStat gold (−$398, magic 57502) AND the MT4 GridStat gold — from the SAME event: Goldminer faded an overbought surge that turned out to be a real breakout → grid stacked 4 legs → hit the −20% basket floor. Diagnosed the two "obvious" fixes DEAD with his live evidence: (1) per-entry SL doesn't help (the grid overrides it, stacks to floor anyway — even the MT5 build has UseEntrySL and still floored); (2) the D1 trend filter doesn't help — his MT4 gold HAS `UseDailyTrendFilter=true` and took the SAME loss (the D1-50 LAGS: breakout erupts out of a prior downtrend, daily still sloping down → filter reads "bearish → sell allowed" → waves it into the breakout). So the floor-hit is confirmed INTRINSIC — it IS the 20% DD figure. Only two honest responses: accept+size, OR attack it with the recovery-ride. User chose: build the recovery ride for MT4.**

**BUILT — `Marius GridStat GOLD v1.0.mq4` build 2026-07-03-Q (compiled CLEAN 0 err / 1 pre-existing warning via XM MT4 metaeditor CLI + Start-Process -Wait; ea/ synced). All new levers DEFAULT-OFF → base config byte-identical until toggled.** The recovery ride = fib C's mechanism adapted to GridStat's SINGLE-DIRECTION grid: when a basket floats deep against a SUSTAINED trend, STOP adding grid legs to the loser and RIDE the winning (trend) side on subsequent Goldminer signals — overriding session/D1/win-rate gates — growing it (Fibonacci ladder, same as the grid) until the WHOLE book (losing basket + hedge + ride) climbs back to +`RecoveryTargetUSD`, then close everything in profit. The −20% catastrophic floor (now including the ride) stays the V-reversal backstop.
- **New inputs (default-OFF/neutral):** `UseRecoveryRide=false`, `RecoveryTriggerPct=12.0` (arm when combined float ≤ −12% balance; must be < BasketMaxLossPct=20 or it never fires), `RecoveryTargetUSD=30.0`, `MaxRideLegs=6`, `MaxRideLotsTotal=0.60`.
- **New globals:** `g_recovering`, `g_rideDir` (= opposite the losing basket = the trend direction).
- **New fn `OpenRideLeg(dir)`:** opens a `_RCV`-tagged leg in the winning direction, sized with the grid's Fibonacci ladder indexed by ride count (so the winning side RAMPS UP to EXCEED the losing basket → continued trend nets the book positive), bounded by MaxRideLegs + MaxRideLotsTotal. Called from OnTick's recovery branch.
- **OnTick restructured:** Goldminer computed BEFORE the session/D1 gates (no side effects → base behaviour unchanged when not recovering) so the recovery branch can override them; while `g_recovering`, only winning-side ride legs open, then return.
- **ManageGrid:** loop now separates `_RCV` legs (rideLots/rideFloat/rideCount); combinedFloat + oneR include the ride; ARM block (combinedFloat ≤ −RecoveryTriggerPct% & nLevels≥2 & !rideOpen) sets g_recovering + g_rideDir; RECOVERY-EXIT block closes all at +RecoveryTargetUSD; grid-expansion gated on `!g_recovering` (stop feeding the loser); floor + nLevels==0 paths reset g_recovering/g_rideDir. **Restart-safe:** AdoptOpenTickets re-arms from any open `_RCV` legs. Needs a HEDGING account (ride is opposite-direction while basket open).
- **Ordering (verified):** as float deepens it ARMS at −12% BEFORE the floor at −20% → the ride gets an 8% window; on a V-reversal the floor still catches the whole book.

**>>> A/B RESULT (2026-07-06, both every-tick GOLD M5 Dec2025–Jun2026, $1,000 deposit, modelling quality 59.5% [LOW — grid path-sensitive, absolutes shaky, but the DELTA is huge], reports in Downloads: `StrategyTester = false.htm` [OFF] / `StrategyTester = true.htm` [ON]) — RECOVERY ON IS DECISIVELY WORSE. The documented chop-whipsaw risk MATERIALIZED.**
| metric | Run A (recovery OFF) | Run B (recovery ON, 12%/$30/6legs/0.6lots) |
|---|---|---|
| Net | **+$5,080** | **+$2,816 (−45%)** |
| PF | **2.66** | **1.59** |
| Max DD | **31.2% ($1,850)** | **43.5% ($1,938)** |
| Trades | 151 | 161 (+10) |
| Loss trades | 43 | 54 (+11) |
| Worst streak | 5 / −$1,305 | **10 / −$1,969** |
| Gross profit | $8,138 | $7,627 (−$510) |
| Gross loss | −$3,057 | **−$4,811 (+$1,754)** |

- **DIAGNOSIS (airtight from the gross split): recovery ON almost entirely ADDED LOSSES (+$1,754 gross loss) while barely touching gross profit (−$510) → the ride legs were net LOSERS, it did NOT rescue.** The +10 trades ≈ the ride legs, and loss-trades rose +11 (win-trades even fell 108→107) → ~every ride leg lost. It also DOUBLED the worst losing streak (5/−$1,305 → 10/−$1,969).
- **ROOT CAUSE = REGIME, not parameters.** The baseline floored ONCE on 2026-05-06 (a BUY basket, −$1,144, as gold DIPPED) then gold BOUNCED and the account recovered. With recovery ON that basket armed at −12%, stopped adding buys, and rode SELLS (betting the dip continues) — then gold bounced → the sells lost too → it floored TWICE (May-7 −$1,050, May-12 −$692) in a 10-loss streak. **Early-May 2026 gold was a dip-then-BOUNCE (mean-reversion), not a sustained trend. The recovery ride is a TREND-CONTINUATION bet; it only rescues when the flooring move CONTINUES. Gold's floor-hits are often reversions (gold ≈ intraday random-walk) → the ride whipsaws and deepens the loss.** This is EXACTLY the OOS chop-whipsaw risk flagged at build time (fib C: "rides trends, whipsaws in chop") — the result validates the caveat, it isn't a surprise.
- **WHY parameter-only optimization likely CAN'T fix it (honest, low prior):** no trigger/target/lot combo separates "sustained breakout" (ride helps — the original MT5 −$398 event) from "dip-bounce" (ride hurts). The rescue math forces a dead end — to net positive on trend continuation the ride must EXCEED the basket (0.6 vs 0.3 lots), but that same oversizing bleeds 2× fast on a whipsaw; shrink it enough to stop the whipsaw and you've turned the ride off (≈ baseline). Expect the best sweep cell ≈ recovery-OFF.
- **>>> FABLE 2nd OPINION (2026-07-06) — VERDICT = DROP THE RIDE; don't sweep, don't build the trend-gate. Sharper than the agent's own diagnosis on the DECISIVE point.** The ride is negative-EV in EVERY regime, for 4 structural reasons, and — the killer — there was NOTHING in this window to rescue:
  - **(a) Arming DISABLES the actual edge.** Baseline floored only ONCE in 6 months → nearly every basket that touched −12% was recovered BY THE GRID. Arming stops adding grid legs at exactly the depth the proven recovery mechanism is doing its job → swaps the edge out for a worse mechanism. The +11 loss-trades/10-loss-streak are that swap, not just whipsaw.
  - **(b) The ride ENTERS AT EXHAUSTION by construction.** Ride legs fire on Goldminer (a %R surge-COMPLETION detector) in the trend direction → on a proven mean-reverter it sells the local lows of the dip = worst possible entry for a continuation bet. "~every ride leg lost" is what that predicts.
  - **(c) Exit asymmetry is a coin-flip-to-floor even when RIGHT.** Armed ~−$600 deficit, full ride net 0.3 lots (~$30/$1 gold) → needs ~$21 more continuation to hit +$30, but a ~$13 bounce against the fully-built ride bleeds straight into the now −$1,000 floor. Gold bounces $10–13 intraday constantly. No ride size fixes +$30-vs-−$1,000 on a random walk.
  - **(d) THE KILLER FACT the agent stepped past: there was ZERO sustained-breakout floor-hit in this window.** The baseline's only floor-hit (May-6, −$1,144) was ITSELF a dip-that-BOUNCED — the floor cut a RECOVERABLE basket at the bottom. The intervention was built to rescue an event that DIDN'T OCCUR; a PERFECT trend-gate's best case here = exactly the baseline. You cannot demonstrate a positive from this data even in principle.
  - **On the trend-gate = LIPSTICK:** perfect gate → reproduces baseline (nothing to win); imperfect → loses; target event is n≈1/6mo on 59.5% data = fitting one May incident; and the project ALREADY proved "you cannot classify bounce-vs-sustained AT the adverse extreme — every trend signal points the wrong way there" (fib C conditional-freeze, same D1-lag). New-extremes confirmation is WORSE (arms at max extension = feeds defect b).
  - **The transplant post-mortem (why fib C's 8/8 didn't carry):** fib C PYRAMIDED the already-WINNING side of an EXISTING hedged book (confirmed direction, positive basis, overriding filters that were BLOCKING a hedge that should exist). GridStat's version INITIATES fresh counter-exposure at ZERO basis at signal-EXHAUSTION prices. **Same name, opposite trade.**
- **>>> THE ONE LIVE THREAD Fable surfaced (the real pivot): the observed loss mode is "floor FIRES on a RECOVERABLE DIP", NOT "breakout never returns" → the interesting question isn't riding trends, it's whether the −20% floor is 2–3 pts TOO TIGHT (cutting baskets that would recover).** That's a ONE-RUN question. Honest engineering lever if any = DEEP-LEG SHAPING (a `MaxFibMult` analog — smaller notional at the worst prices → −12% baskets are smaller in $ → floor fires later/never), entry-side, no new exposure, keeps the recovery mechanism intact (expectation still low per [[mae-separation-not-maxdd-reduction]], but aimed at the RIGHT target). Freeze-hedge 1:1 (neutralize not overtake) dominates the 2× ride but fib C v3 showed it just reshapes WHEN you pay (dead capital + same unsolvable release-timing) → doesn't beat accept+size.
- **RECOMMENDATION (agent + Fable agree): DROP the ride, keep the flag default-OFF (code stays as dead research). The −20% floor-hit is the insurance premium of a vol-selling grid (project concluded this before: fib C 19h/19t-e). Budget the tail, SIZE to it, spend engineering on the PROVEN positive-EV levers = breadth × portfolio-DD-headroom (~9.4% combined vs 20% gold-alone = ~2× size free) × capital [[sweat-proven-edge-over-new-hunt]].** Only sanctioned run in this area = a 100%-quality re-run of the BASELINE to confirm the May floor-fire was a bounce casualty → if the floor's only observed failure mode is cutting recoverable dips, the real one-run question is whether the floor is too tight (or wants deep-leg shaping). CAVEAT throughout: 59.5% quality + 22 mismatched-chart errors = shaky absolutes (−45% delta too big to be a data artifact, direction solid). Live MT4 gold + whole book UNTOUCHED; build 2026-07-03-Q unchanged.

**THE PROFESSIONAL-ARCHITECTURE ANSWER (logged):** MT5 = retail/BUNDLED (your strategy in the broker's terminal, MQL5, ONE broker's spreads/fills, no venue choice = the cost wall that killed FX-fade/market-making/spread-grid/crypto). PROFESSIONAL = UNBUNDLED: your own engine (Python/C++: data→signal→risk/OMS→execution→VENUE) + DMA/ECN/exchange API where you CHOOSE liquidity, post limits as a MAKER (earn the spread/rebate), see the real order book. **The pro edge is CHOOSING EXECUTION, not speed — it removes the retail-cost wall.** CRUCIAL for trend specifically: our past trend died on MT5 CFD cost (crypto-trend swap −2659; CFD spread+swap on multi-day holds) — on real FUTURES (IB) or a crypto EXCHANGE those costs largely vanish → the pro venue could turn "mediocre trend" into real. Trend is the IDEAL first pro build: low-frequency (latency irrelevant, no HFT infra) but needs BREADTH (20+ markets = what a multi-venue engine gives, a single MT5 broker can't). On-ramps: **Interactive Brokers** (`ib_insync`; global futures/FX/stocks, real DMA, the multi-asset standard — more capital + a real OMS build) / **crypto exchange** (`ccxt` Binance/Bybit/Kraken; easiest API, maker rebates, 24/7, low capital, crypto-only) / **cTrader/FIX ECN** (FX raw spreads, resurrects FX-fade). Honest cost: a real engineering step up (OMS, reconnects, partial fills, HARD risk kill-switches — your code places orders with no broker guardrails; months not days).

**>>> HOW WE DECIDE THE VENUE (the framework — the user's open question; answer = the venue is an OUTPUT of the validated edge, not an input):** validate the trend signal in Python first (tick data is easy to get; model REAL cost; venue-agnostic), then the edge's OWN characteristics pick the venue: (1) **WHICH INSTRUMENTS survive** — the cost-first screen ranks markets by net trend edge; the surviving universe chooses the venue (index/commodity/rate futures→IB; crypto→exchange; FX majors→ECN). (2) **COST STRUCTURE that keeps it positive** — trend = multi-day holds → financing/SWAP + spread dominate (not latency); futures (no swap, pay commission+roll) usually beat CFDs (swap-bled); crypto perps have funding (±), spot has none → pick the lowest holding-cost venue for the surviving instruments. (3) **FREQUENCY/LATENCY** — trend is low-freq → latency irrelevant → no co-lo/HFT → choose on cost+breadth not speed (a future MARKET-MAKING edge WOULD flip this to maker-rebate+low-latency venues). (4) **MAKER vs TAKER** — trend crosses the spread at entry/exit but holds long → taker cost amortizes over a big move → taker is fine (maker matters for HFT/market-making, not trend). (5) **CAPITAL** — IB/futures+prime = higher minimums/margin per contract; crypto exchange = low minimum → match venue to committed capital. (6) **API/library maturity + reliability + jurisdiction** (`ccxt`/`ib_insync`). **DEFAULT for a diversified TREND book = Interactive Brokers (real futures)**: tight commissions, no CFD swap, the broadest liquid universe (the breadth trend needs), mature Python API; a crypto sleeve via ccxt ONLY if the screen shows crypto-trend survives its funding cost. The screen + real-cost model decide the venue; never pick it on vibes. **NEXT ACTION = build the multi-market trend screen in Python (free/easy tick or daily data, REAL per-instrument cost modeled, ATR-trailing Donchian/MA-cross, robustness across params + first/second-half + diversified-portfolio Sharpe) → the surviving universe tells us the venue → THEN wire the winner to that venue's API.** (Reusable prior art: edge_trend2.py / edge_trend_portfolio.py found a ~0.6-Sharpe 20-market trend portfolio, uncorrelated — the honest baseline to beat with proper futures cost.)

### Session 19t-c — 2026-06-23 (BREAK-EVEN recovery sizing — concept taken from a zone-recovery video, built as an A/B lever)

**User shared a zone-recovery hedging video. Verdict: the STRATEGY = fib C (same no-stop recovery hedge, same admitted margin-call tail — nothing new). The ONE useful concept = its recovery LOT-SIZING FORMULA (break-even guarantee) as an alternative to fib C's Fibonacci ladder: size each recovery leg = (RR+1)/RR × (opposite-side lots − same-side lots) × CostBuffer = the MINIMAL leg that makes the basket net-positive at the next favourable move, COST INCLUDED (the explicit ×1.1 cost buffer is a sound discipline). More principled than Fibonacci's heuristic multiples; at higher RR grows gentler → potentially smaller per-basket tail = the gentler-ladder thesis ([[MaxFibMult]]) with a clean derivation.** BUILT into fib C MT5 (build FIBC-MT5-2026-06-23-E, compiled 0/0, ea/+live synced): `RecoverySizeMode` (0=Fibonacci default UNCHANGED; 1=break-even) + `RecoveryRR`(3.0) + `RecoveryCostBuffer`(1.1) + SideLots() helper + CONFIG print. In mode 1, one-sided trend (D1 filter on) → formula floors to base = UNIFORM lots (gentlest ladder); two-sided/hedged → sizes the recovery leg to overcome the opposite side (adaptive). **>>> A/B: every-tick GOLD M5 $3k hedging — Run A RecoverySizeMode=0 (Fib control), Run B =1/RR3.0/buf1.1; compare net/PF/equity-DD%/Recovery; then sweep RecoveryRR{2,3,5} (higher=gentler).** Hypothesis: leaner ladder → lower DD at similar/slightly-lower net = better DD-per-dollar than Fibonacci. HONEST: a SIZING refinement, NOT a tail fix (even leanest ladder margin-calls in a long one-way move — the video admits it); real tail containment is still the vol-regime/overextension filters. NOTE: the video method = standard zone-recovery ("the cog"); textbook, not new; only the cost-aware break-even sizing was worth taking.

**FEASIBILITY of a tick-faithful Python GRID SIMULATOR (the only honest way to measure grid P&L in Python — the grid is intrabar-path-sensitive, so bars/1-min-OHLC = phantom, must be ticks):**
- **TICK DATA IS REACHABLE via the MT5 Python API — 19n's "XM serves no ticks" was WRONG.** It was the async-download quirk: `copy_ticks_from` (count-based) works immediately; `copy_ticks_range` returns None / `(-1,'Terminal: Call failed')` on the FIRST call (kicks off async download) then SUCCEEDS on retry (OILCash returned 43,803 ticks for one day; ~5–6M for gold's 6-mo window = fits memory). FIX = retry loop w/ sleep. FRICTION: `mt5.initialize()` ≈ 60s; bulk 6-mo tick pull is slow (timed out >5min on-demand; likely cacheable after first download).
- **PARITY TEST built (`gridsim_parity.py`): recompute the EA-logged indicator/signal values in Python from M5/D1 bars and diff vs the shadow DB's EXACT MT5 values (gridstat_setups_gold_20260609.csv, 335 gold signals).** RESULT:
  | input | Python vs MT5 | verdict |
  |---|---|---|
  | WPR oscillator (Goldminer core) | med err **0.025** | ✅ EXACT (signal ports faithfully — consistent w/ the old 50/50 MT5-vs-MT4) |
  | MA angle | med err **0.22°** | ✅ matched (a few sign-flips at near-vertical slopes = cosmetic) |
  | **ADX** | med err **8.2**, max 32 | ❌ does NOT match MT5 `iADX` |
  | **ATR(D1)** | med err **~11 (~10%+)** | ❌ does NOT match MT5 `iATR` on D1 |
- **CONCLUSION: a full Python grid-P&L sim CANNOT reproduce the $7,908 anchor without reverse-engineering MT5's exact iADX + iATR(D1) (incl. broker D1 server-timezone bar boundaries).** ADX gates entries (`IsTrendStrong`=ADX>25 → an 8-pt error flips the gate for any signal with true ADX 17–33) and ATR(D1) sets the ±0.8·ATR barrier/grid geometry (~10% error shifts every SL/TP/spacing) → entries AND outcomes diverge → "close but wrong" = the 1-min-OHLC phantom. Replicating the SIGNAL is solved; replicating the EXECUTION-geometry indicators is the wall.

**DECISION (user, this session): go the LIGHTER FAITHFUL path now — the Ch.17 vol-regime / structural-break filter for the momentum book — and validate the winner in MT5 every-tick; DEFER the full tick sim to a later stage (the iADX/iATR reverse-engineering is the prerequisite if ever resumed).** KEY ENABLER: the vol-regime analysis does NOT need indicator parity — it can use the shadow DB's EXACT MT5-logged features (adx/atr/ma_angle/wpr/runup/mfe_r) + add Python bar-derived Ch.17 features (frac-diff, vol-regime ratio, SADF-lite explosiveness); MT5 every-tick remains the P&L arbiter → no phantom risk. For a size-up (DD) lever the worst basket itself must be in the filtered set; on gold it isn't. (A regime feature can be a real edge on the distribution yet useless for the max-DD/sizing goal.)

Both LONG & SHORT 29% (NOT a sign error — logic verified correct). MFE/MAE chart: favorable excursions compressed, adverse dominate = positions underwater immediately. ROOT CAUSE: the hard SL sits at ~4×vol (~8 pips) but at 22:00–00:00 SERVER time the spread spikes to many multiples of that → the position is past its SL the instant it opens. 3% win is too extreme for a directional error (that'd be ~30-40%); time-of-day-dependent win% = the spread signature. The Python gates (CPCV/holdout/cost-stress) all measured MID-PRICE R with a FLAT COST_R=0.15-0.30 that could NOT see the rollover spread spike → live every-tick (real spread) is the arbiter that caught it, as designed. RESULT: ML gate gross/mid **+0.541R/77% win** (mid-price edge REAL, matches research) → **NET real spread −0.106R, avg spread cost 0.647R/trade (4× the old flat 0.15R), 0/6 folds, DSR 0%**; holdout NET real spread −0.131R FAIL. → reproduces the live −$2,996 disaster in Python; the cost model is validated. SPREAD-GATE SWEEP (the salvage): keep entry-spread ≤ p25 (0.25R) → best net **+0.014R** (≈ZERO, noise) ; p50 +0.007; p10 +0.010. **→ market-order rollover-fade NOT tradeable on XM retail spreads even spread-gated; best subset ≈ 0.** Confirmed 3 ways (MT5, real-spread CPCV, real-spread holdout). Least-bad symbols = the tight majors GBPUSD/EURUSD (~+0.05-0.18R net), worst = EURGBP/NZDUSD/AUDJPY. **THE ALIGNED INSIGHT: the mid-price edge is LARGE (+0.54R); the entire loss is CROSSING the spread with MARKET orders. That's the next aligned test (model limit fills in Python: entry≈mid via limit, charge only exit/adverse-selection, not full round-trip spread). Caveat: adverse selection (limit fills exactly when the move continues) + non-fills + MT5 limit-fill modeling is unreliable.** Also viable: a RAW-SPREAD/ECN broker (cuts the 0.647R substantially — can't test without that data). afml_engine.py now the real-spread engine (costR/entry_costR per event; spread-gate + LIMIT-execution sweeps added).
**LIMIT-EXECUTION SWEEP (2b) — FLIPS THE CONCLUSION: the edge is dead as a spread-TAKER, strongly alive as a spread-MAKER.** net = gross_mid(+0.541) − f·spread(0.647): pay 100%(market)=−0.106 / 75%=+0.056 / **50%=+0.218R (1,067 totR, 75% win)** / 25%=+0.379 / 0%(ideal limit)=+0.541R. → **if execution pays ≤50% of round-trip spread, net +0.22R over ~4,900 trades = a large real edge.** The whole loss was TAKING liquidity (market orders) at the rollover; a reversion edge should PROVIDE it. HONEST CAVEAT: the sweep applies a uniform discount to the SAME trades — real limit/maker execution adds ADVERSE SELECTION (fills preferentially when the move continues) + NON-FILLS → realized sits BELOW the clean f-numbers; +0.22R@f=0.5 is optimistic, only live fills settle it. TWO ALIGNED PATHS to pay ≤50% spread: **(A) RAW-SPREAD/ECN broker** (cuts the round-trip spread ~half directly, NO adverse-selection confound — the CLEANER test; keep the existing market-order FXFade EA, just on a raw account; per-symbol already shows tightest majors GBPUSD/EURUSD slightly POSITIVE even on XM-standard spread → on raw they'd be solid + more symbols join). **(B) LIMIT/MAKER FXFade EA** (post a fade limit at the over-extension at the CUSUM event, short expiry; higher ceiling +0.22-0.54R but adverse-selection risk; MT5 limit-fill backtest is optimistic → demo is the arbiter). RECOMMEND A first (simpler, lower-risk, directly tests the spread-halving thesis); B is the higher-ceiling build. The momentum-grid book (gold/silver/oil) is unaffected — it trades liquid all-day hours, no rollover-spread problem.

### Session 19m — 2026-06-19 (the "ride gold winners" question — DEFINITIVELY resolved with data + the answer = a SEPARATE trend engine)

**User saw GOLD GridStat demo open a sell, bank +$100, then gold kept falling 4260→4181 ("didn't capitalise on the trend"). Asked: how to capture continuation; does López de Prado have a solution?** This is the project's most-tested tension (trailing S16 $6k→$1.7k, asym-TP S17 $5,192→$893, lock-trail S12-14, regime-exit S10 — 4 prior ride-attempts all FAILED). This session MEASURED WHY, decisively.

**THE DECISIVE DATA (analysis_data scanner snapshot, GOLD grisk=4, 990 signals, UNBARRIERED 288-bar/24h horizon — removes the triple-barrier ±0.8ATR measurement cap):** MFE (peak favorable move) median 0.38R / mean **0.47R**; only **12% run >1R, 1% >2R, 0% >3R**; horizon_r (where it ACTUALLY sits at +24h) mean **+0.04R** → **the average gold signal peaks at ~0.47R then GIVES BACK 0.43R, mean-reverting to ~flat within the day.** The +$100 fixed exit (~0.6R) HARVESTS that transient peak before it evaporates; riding/trailing = watching the peak melt to zero. **This is the MATH behind all 4 failed ride-attempts — they weren't mistuned, they were fighting gold's intraday mean-reversion.** (Entry-feature check on gridstat_setups_gold: ADX≥35 → 60% win but meanMFE 0.53 vs 0.49, MA-alignment no help → the 12% real runners are NOT identifiable at entry → no in-harvester rule rides them without bleeding the 88% back to 0.) The user's chart = one of the 12% trend days (the ~1R move felt huge but is ~1 ATR); recency/survivorship bias confirmed.

**THE ANSWER (López de Prado framing + project's own Session-13 lesson): you CANNOT get both jobs from one exit — they are mathematically opposed.** Harvester = high win%, small winners, banks the transient peak, wins the 88% chop. Trend-follower = LOW win% (~35%), many small ATR-stop losses, holds the 12% runner days for big R. The fixed +$100 exit is PROVABLY CORRECT *for the harvester* — don't corrupt it (gold stays [[gold-fixed-exit-dont-ride-winners]], now with the mechanism proven, not just observed). To capture continuation → build a SEPARATE gold TREND stream (own magic, runs alongside): breakout/momentum trigger (Donchian or Goldminer) + ATR chandelier trailing stop, accept low win% for big-R winners. MEASURE honestly (does catching the 12% pay for the 88% small stops?) → CPCV → deploy as another PORTFOLIO stream. KEY: harvester & trend-follower are NEGATIVELY correlated by construction (harvester wins chop, trend wins trends) → ideal for the HRP book (Ch.16). = roadmap Phase 2 (diversify edge TYPES) made concrete. NOTE: MaxConcurrentBaskets (S11) also now removes the turnover-collapse that killed trailing, so a conditional partial-ride is *technically* viable too — but the data says the juice isn't there (avg move reverts), so the SEPARATE-engine path is the right one, not patching the harvester.

| trade-all | 746 | 55% | +52.15 | +0.070 | 83% |

| **ML meta-gate (p≥0.55)** | 137 | **61%** | +19.99 | **+0.146** | 83% |
| ML-sized (Ch.10 prob→size) | 292 | 58% | +3.25 | +0.011 | 83% |

**FINDINGS:** (1) **Meta-labeling WORKS as a selector** — ML lifts win 55→61% & per-trade edge +0.070→+0.146R (best per-trade of all methods); the book's core idea is validated. (2) **But it does NOT beat the fingerprint on TOTAL return** — too picky (137 vs 329 trades), turnover loss > quality gain (+20R < +34R < trade-all's +52R) = the SAME turnover>edge lesson as gold grisk-4/asym-TP/trailing. (3) **Probability-sizing HURT** (+0.011 avgR) — RF probabilities cluster near 0.5 (weak separation) → bet sizes shrink to ~0. (4) **DSR gut-check:** ML OOS per-trade Sharpe +0.203, PSR(SR>0)=98.9% (edge is REAL) but Deflated-SR≈0% vs best-of-50-trials noise (edge is SMALL; small edge + heavy backtesting → trust LIVE forward-test, not more backtests). DSR's SR* is a rough 6-fold-dispersion proxy (conservative). (5) **Feature importance (in-sample):** wpr dominates (0.24) > atr/adx/runup/ma_angle/bars_in_band; **big_move=0.000 (re-confirms S4 it's noise); symbol_code=0.02 → edge is the SAME shape across gold/silver/oil = empirical justification for POOLING + a cross-sectional EA.** VERDICT: ML meta-model is sample-starved on 746 events; path to making it WIN = MORE instruments (10–20 → thousands of events) + ML as a SIZING overlay within the fingerprint-approved set, not a standalone gate. Confirms the design thesis: real but modest edge; turnover + portfolio matter more than model cleverness.

MECHANISM: the Fib recovery grid catches mean-reversion only when an adverse move stays within the grid's recovery span; the fixed −30% floor is a CONSTANT blind to vol. When vol regime-changes 10×, one day's move (310pt Feb4-5) exceeds the grid's span AND trips the −30% floor BEFORE reversion → floor dumps at the exact bottom (4778), gold reverts to 5048 in 4 days = proof it would've recovered. Principle = scale to volatility (triple-barrier scales EXITS to ATR; this scales ENTRIES + FLOOR to the vol regime). PROOF IT WORKS in our own logs: GridStat risk-norm **vol-skip "correctly blocked the Feb-explosion trades" on silver (Session 19)** — a working structural-break filter already in the codebase, just never wired into fib C. A vol-regime filter is RISK MANAGEMENT, not signal-selection → philosophically OK for the hedge EA (unlike the rejected GridStat setup-gating).

### Session 17 — 2026-06-06

**MT5 PORT + SIGNAL LAB.** Building `Marius GridStat MT5 v1.0.mq5` in the MT5 terminal (`...\BB16F565FAAA6B23A20C26C49416FF05\MQL5\Experts\`). Two drivers: (1) move the validated GridStat method onto MT5; (2) MT5 has no Goldminer indicator, so it must be reimplemented natively — which is the chance to make it *better*, not just ported. This is also the long-pending **"signal lab"** (pluggable/measurable entry signal) made real: a shadow-only Stage-1 harness that measures signal edge on ANY instrument before any execution code is written.

**Goldminer analysis (reconstructed — the indicator the user supplied):** an *adaptive-period Williams %R swing detector*.
- Volatility base: `avgRange` = mean(|High−Low|) over 10 bars.
- Two accelerators shorten the WPR period when the market jumps: a **gap** (`|Open−PrevClose| ≥ 2.0×avgRange` → period 3) or a **fast move** (`|Close[i+3]−Close[i]| ≥ 4.6×avgRange` → period 4); otherwise period = `RISK*2+3`.
- Oscillator `val = 100 − |%R|` (0 = oversold … 100 = overbought).
- **Signal = full traverse through the neutral band:** BUY when val is now > upper band AND the prior band-breakout was below the lower band (surged oversold→overbought); SELL on the mirror. Bands: upper = `RISK+67`, lower = `33−RISK`.
- The MT4 EAs read its two arrow buffers as `ggreen` (buy) / `gred` (sell). The single `RISK` input = the EA's `grisk` (gold 7, silver 14).

**The "many ideas" to make it explore more options (reconstructed full list):**
- ✅ **Decouple the RISK knob** — biggest one. `RISK` ties period + upper + lower together; the optimizer can't explore the space. Split into `GM_Period`, `GM_UpperBand`, `GM_LowerBand` (each `0` = auto-derive from `grisk`, so old behaviour is the default).
- ✅ **Expose the volatility-acceleration constants** `GM_GapMult` (2.0), `GM_FastMult` (4.6) as inputs — these silently control how reactive the signal is on jumps; per-instrument they likely differ.
- ✅ **Instrument every signal** — log `big_move` (did gap/fast fire?) and `wpr` (the value at entry) to the setups CSV → AFML feature-importance: replace the hand-picked DIR|SESSION|ATR fingerprint with data-driven cuts.
- ⬜ **Asymmetric thresholds** — separate buy vs sell bands (gold/silver have directional bias: buys win in NY, sells in LDN/OVL).
- ⬜ **Signal-strength score** — penetration depth past the band + traverse speed (how few bars the swing took) → a continuous confidence number feeding the AFML bet-sizing layer, not a binary arrow.
- ⬜ **`bars_in_band` feature** — log how long the swing took (a 5-bar traverse vs a 200-bar one are different signals); optionally cap the max traverse length as an input.
- ⬜ **True-ATR volatility base** — replace the crude 10-bar High−Low mean (or expose its lookback) so gap/fast detection is consistent across instruments.
- ⬜ **MTF confirmation** — compute Goldminer on the entry TF + a higher TF; fire only when aligned.

**Built — `Marius GridStat MT5 v1.0.mq5` Stage 1 (build `MT5-2026-06-05-S1`), shadow-only:**
- Native MQL5 (handles + `CopyBuffer`; `iMA/iADX/iATR` handles in `OnInit`; manual WPR via `iHighest`/`iLowest`). Faithful port of the swing logic + the ✅ enhancements above.
- Inputs: `grisk=4` (auto-default), `GM_Period/GM_UpperBand/GM_LowerBand=0` (auto), `GM_GapMult=2.0`, `GM_FastMult=4.6`, `goldminershift=1`. Same entry filters as gold/silver (MA-angle 20, ADX 25, D1 trend, session 9–23).
- Triple-barrier shadow (BarrierATR 0.8, TimeBarrier 288) → writes `gridstat_setups_oil.csv` (Common\Files) with 14 cols incl. `mfe_r`, `big_move`, `wpr`. `OnTester` returns `g_shadowTotalR` for the grisk optimizer (same pattern as MT4 build O).

**OIL first shadow reads + two MT5 tester gotchas (compiled CLI clean, 0 errors):**
- **Build S2** (BarrierATR 0.8, the only data available at the time = Dec-2025→Jun-2026 bull window): 248 signals, +0.087 R/trade, +21.6 R; BUY +0.136 R, SELL −0.182 R. Looked *better than gold's raw shadow* — but that was a cherry-picked single-regime window.
- **Tester gotcha #1 — chart depth ≠ tester depth.** Chart scrolled to Nov 2024 but tester started Dec 2025. Cause: "Every tick based on real ticks" needs `.tkc` files; OILCash ticks start Jan 2025; bars (`.hcc`) go to 2021. **Fix: Modelling = "1 minute OHLC"** (uses M1 bars → reaches 2021) for the shadow lab — tick precision isn't needed to measure signal edge, and the deeper range gives regime diversity.
- **Tester gotcha #2 — Forward truncates a single run.** With Forward=1/3, a single (non-optimization) run only covered the forward third (started exactly at the 2024.08.13 split). **Fix: Forward = No.** Also MT5 has no "Use date" checkbox — the **Date: Custom period** dropdown is the equivalent.
- **Tester gotcha #3 — source default ≠ tester input.** Recompiling with `BarrierATRMultiplier=0.5` did NOT change the run; the tester persists its own input values (still 0.8, verified: barrier/ATR ratio = 0.8 in the CSV). **Fix: set the value in the tester Inputs tab,** not just in code.
- **Build S2 longer run (2024.08→2026, but barrier still 0.8 due to gotcha #3): 902 signals, the verdict flips.** Per-year R: **2024 −2.66, 2025 −0.59, 2026 +24.45.** ALL of oil's "edge" is the 2026 bull run; 2024–25 flat-to-negative. `big_move` fired 1/902 (gap/fast detector dead on oil — multipliers need lowering or it's uninformative). TIME exits ~70%.

I never did the selection step on oil, and never built the single biggest lever I had myself identified: **AFML feature-importance (Improvement #2).** The triple-barrier METHOD is instrument-agnostic; what's unproven on oil is whether a *selectable subset* has edge. **Before Stage 2, user asked: what ELSE in the Goldminer can be optimized to make even GOLD more profitable + synergize with triple-barrier? (Reconstructed the lost "all parameters" discussion — FULL MAP below.)**

GOLDMINER = adaptive-period Williams-%R swing detector. Every explorable lever:
- **The RISK knot** — one input drives THREE: period=`R*2+3`, upper=`R+67`, lower=`33-R` (forces bands symmetric, midpoint locked 50). ✅ decoupled in S3 (GM_Period/Upper/Lower); **band asymmetry now reachable but not yet optimized** (gold is directional — buys in NY uptrend, sells in downtrend).
- **Volatility engine** — `avgRange`=mean(|H-L|) over hard-10 bars; feeds the big-move detector. Levers: lookback, method (true ATR/median), and **aligning it with the barrier's ATR(D1)** for coherent labeling (🆕 not built).
- **Big-move accelerator** (the built-in runner detector) — gap `|Open-PrevClose|≥2.0·avgRange`→period3, fast `|Close[i+3]-Close[i]|≥4.6·avgRange`→period4. **Was DEAD (~0 fires) because bar-to-bar gaps don't exist on continuous CFDs.** Redefining the gap as close-to-close ACTIVATES it → catches breakouts earlier, rides momentum into the upper barrier.
- **Swing/traverse logic** — signal needs a full extreme→band→opposite-extreme traverse. **`bars_in_band`** (how long the swing took: 5-bar snap vs 200-bar grind) = strong selection feature + quality filter (🆕).
- **Triple-barrier synergy** — barrier width (per-symbol), **asymmetric barriers** (upper>lower lets winners run → lifts avg R directly; AFML), time-barrier horizon.

**Build S4 (compiles clean, 0 err/0 warn) — added the 3 highest-leverage levers, all defaulting to current behavior (so the 50/50 GOLD match is preserved by default):**
1. **Asymmetric triple-barrier** — `BarrierATRUpper`/`BarrierATRLower` (0 = symmetric). R is now measured in units of the STOP (lower) distance, so a wider target pays `favDist/advDist` > 1R on a win, −1R on a loss. `CheckPendingBarriers` rewritten for favDist/advDist (symmetric → ±1R exactly as before).
2. **Activatable big-move detector** — `GM_GapMode` (0 = original open-gap MT4-equiv; 1 = close-to-close jump that actually fires on gold/oil). `period_used` already logged to test if accelerated signals run further.
3. **`bars_in_band`** — new CSV column (the traverse length captured from the band-cross scan).
CSV is now 19 cols: …,adx,ma_angle,runup,period_used,bars_in_band. Asymmetric BANDS already available via S3's GM_Upper/LowerBand.

**S4 lever measurements on GOLD (each result is MEASURED, kept for the record):**
- **Baseline (S4 defaults, symmetric ±0.8 ATR, GapMode=0):** 241 signals, win 53.5%, +0.047 R/trade, **+11.30 R total**, 68.5% TIME. Confirms S4 defaults = S3 (didn't break the 50/50). gold raw edge ≈ +0.05 R (matches MT4-era build E).
- **`GM_GapMode=1` (big-move detector) — REJECTED for gold.** Diagnostic run (upper 3.0/lower 0.8/GapMode=1): the 39 big_move=1 signals won only **41%, +0.006 R, MFE 0.38** vs big_move=0 **53%, +0.046 R, MFE 0.44**; accelerated period-3 signals run *less* (MFE median 0.19 vs 0.26). The clever accelerator is noise on gold M5 and pollutes the signal set (241→274). Keep `GM_GapMode=0`. (Instrumentation killed a plausible idea with data — the point of the build.)
- **Asymmetric barriers — gold barely runs, modest lift.** True uncapped MFE: median 0.25R, only 10% reach 1R, 2.6% reach 2R (max 3.49R) → gold is a WEAK runner on M5/24h. Optimal-upper sim peaked ~upper 1.5–1.75R (~+0.05/trade) but on the polluted GapMode=1 set → needs a clean confirm (GapMode=0, upper 1.5/lower 0.8 → gridstat_setups_gold_asym.csv).
- **Asymmetric barriers (upper1.5/lower0.8) — CONFIRMED ~+50% shadow R** on the identical 241 signals: +11.30 → **+16.91 R** (avgR +0.047→+0.070, same ~53% win; winners reaching 1.5R pay 1.875R, losers −1R). The only S4 lever with a real lift. CAVEAT: shadow-R-to-barrier ≠ live P&L — realizing it needs execution to capture 1.5R, and session-16 showed letting-winners-run (trailing) CUT live profit via turnover collapse. Could implement as a WIDER FIXED TP (decisive, unlike trailing) to dodge that — but only a Stage-2 P&L backtest settles it.
- **Feature selection — REJECTED (corrects my earlier "biggest lever" claim).** The ma_angle "mid-band loser" was a tertile-binning artifact; explicit 82–88° band is +1.31R (neutral) and TIME-UNSTABLE: +9.21R first-half (66% win) vs −7.90R second-half (38% win) = overfit/regime-flip, not a real cut. Stacking exclusions only bled total R (11.30→9.99→8.54→4.03 = the gate trap, per [[rigor-drives-sizing-not-gating]]). No execution-independent gold improvement found in the features.
- **Tester gotcha repeated:** the tester persists its own input values — the "defaults" run used BarrierATRMultiplier=0.8 (old saved value), not the source's 0.5. Set values in the Inputs tab. And MetaEditor reverted S4→S3 once ([[metaeditor-buffer-overwrites-disk]]) — edit with it closed, compile via CLI.

**CONCLUSION of the gold-improvement sweep:** of all S4 levers, only **asymmetric barriers** lift gold (and only in shadow-R; execution-dependent). GapMode + feature-cuts rejected with data. The shadow has given all it can → **build Stage 2 execution with TP as a tunable R-multiple**, backtest symmetric (≈$4,951 baseline) vs asymmetric 1.5R-TP, let real P&L + turnover decide. The instrumentation did its job: killed 2 plausible ideas, confirmed 1 (conditionally).

**CONCEPT REFERENCE — triple-barrier is a LABELING/EXIT CHASSIS, not a trigger (AFML Ch.3).** It takes an EVENT (an entry time someone else supplies) and labels the outcome by which barrier hits first: upper(+k×vol TP)/lower(−k×vol SL)/vertical(time cap). It does NOT generate entries → **can't work alone** (alone it's a vol-scaled bracket-exit with a clock applied to nothing). It needs a TRIGGER (the "primary model"; AFML's native one is the CUSUM filter). Why it synergises with Goldminer: they're ORTHOGONAL halves — Goldminer = entry timing, triple-barrier = exit + objective R-label; vol-scaling makes both self-calibrating/transferable; momentum's decay horizon ≈ the vertical/time barrier on a TRENDING instrument; and the R-labels are what enabled the whole AFML stack (grisk tuning, fingerprints, CPCV). Strongest at the MEASUREMENT layer (the live exit we actually use is fixed-$100+grid, which beat running to the barrier). **General rule: a trigger pairs well with triple-barrier when the trigger's edge-horizon matches the barrier's time/vol scale.** Other triggers that fit the same chassis: CUSUM (sampling), breakout/Donchian (trend), mean-reversion (needs ASYMMETRIC barriers + ranging regime), ML primary→meta-labeling (the AFML endgame; our feature-importance-as-sizing is step 1). Architecture = keep triple-barrier fixed, make the TRIGGER pluggable (the "signal lab"). See Session 17 signal-lab plan.

**BUILD PATH / ROADMAP (logged at user request — the investor's "find every profit engine + scan the market" program):**
- **Phase 1 — finish the GOLD momentum engine (IN PROGRESS):** (#1) decoupled Goldminer shadow sweep — GM_Period × GM_UpperBand × GM_LowerBand independently (grisk overridden) → does a combo grisk CAN'T reach beat grisk=4? (#2) feature-importance as SIZING (not gating) — rigor+CPCV on the grisk-4 feature columns, size bets by confidence per [[rigor-drives-sizing-not-gating]]. Then gold is fully exploited.
- **Phase 2 — pluggable triggers (signal lab):** add `TriggerMode` to the chassis (0=Goldminer, 1=CUSUM-momentum/breakout, 2=CUSUM-reversion, 3=mean-reversion RSI/BB/z-score). Mean-reversion needs ASYMMETRIC barriers (stop is the critical one) + LOW-ADX regime filter (inverse of Goldminer's trend gate). Swap trigger only; reuse the entire triple-barrier chassis + feature logging.
- **Phase 3 — TEST/SCAN BENCH (the user's core vision):** a multi-symbol SHADOW scanner EA — ONE backtest run loops a symbol list × engines and logs per-symbol/engine setups CSVs (an EA on one chart CAN read many symbols' closed bars → feasible). Then a Python LEADERBOARD: batch rigor+CPCV over all CSVs → ranked table of symbol × engine × fingerprint by OOS-validated edge. = "scan the market, find the winners." Full symbols×settings×engines brute-force is too large → the scan (fixed sensible settings) NARROWS the field first; then optimize finalists. (Fallback if scanner impractical: symbol-by-symbol — but the scanner makes it one pass.)
- **Phase 4 — optimize + validate winners:** per top symbol×engine, focused per-symbol optimize (grisk/bands/barrier) + EVERY-TICK trade-mode validation (mandatory for grid execution) + CPCV gate → deploy each as its own live stream (own magic).
- **Principle:** diversify EDGE TYPES for uncorrelated growth — momentum harvests TRENDERS (gold, silver); mean-reversion harvests RANGERS (USDCHF, US500, EURCHF — the very instruments REJECTED for momentum because Goldminer was signal-sparse → a hint they don't trend → prime reversion candidates). CUSUM-momentum likely correlates with Goldminer (both momentum) → lowest diversification value; mean-reversion is the prize.
- **Fidelity rule:** SHADOW scan can use bars/1-min OHLC (no grid path-sensitivity in shadow); grid EXECUTION validation MUST be every-tick.

**RUN 2 (mean-reversion, %R-extreme-cross) RESULT — gold control PASSED, but engine OVER-FIRES (no winners crowned).** 350,193 signals (~10× momentum's 37k; ~3000/symbol/grisk = over-firing + heavy label overlap). Confirms the symmetric-MFE/MAE structural call: reversion is a low-Sharpe time-drift, barely above costs. **DECISION: shelve CusumRev as a tiny optional diversifier (bankable but not a pillar); do NOT spend more capital/engineering on it.** PIVOT engineering to the PROVEN momentum book: (1) **gold equity-DD fix (S9→S10, regime-aware basket exit)** to lower the 20% intraday DD → safely size gold bigger [HIGHEST ROI]; (2) Stage-B the momentum candidates (USDCHF/indices/AUDJPY, per-signal means ~ gold's) via grid+every-tick+CPCV. Path to $2–6k/mo = scale+diversify the PROVEN engine, not the marginal one.

**GOLD equity-DD fix BUILT (S9→S10, compiles clean, ea/ synced).** New input `UseRegimeBasketExit` (DEFAULT OFF → Monday's locked config byte-identical). When ON: closes a LOSING basket when the D1 trend flips AGAINST it (regime change = the one-way bleed) — caps the tail before the −20% floor → lower intraday equity DD → size the proven engine bigger. VALIDATE every-tick (grisk=4, UseRegimeBasketExit=true, Dec2025–Jun2026) vs the $7,908 / 20.2%-equity-DD baseline: keep ON only if equity DD drops materially WITHOUT denting net (if regime exits cut recoveries and crater net → leave OFF). **RESULT: REJECTED.** Regime-exit ON: net $5,988 (−24%), equity DD 16.07% (−4pp), PF 2.81, balance DD 6.9%. Return/DD WORSE (391→373); even sizing the lower-DD version up 1.26× = $7,545 < $7,908. So it lowers DD but costs more net than it saves → **flag stays OFF; gold locked = S9/S10-default = $7,908.** 4th failed attempt to beat baseline gold (asym-TP, feature-sizing, regime-exit, +decoupled signal all lost) → **gold grisk=4 is definitively the ceiling; its 20% equity DD is INTRINSIC to the grid recovery and not engineerable away. **Key findings:**
- EA fingerprint logic (confirmed from source): `DIRECTION|SESSION|ATR_REGIME`. Session by broker hour: `<9` ASIA, `<14` LDN, `<18` OVL, `≥18` NY. ATR regime vs avg: `>1.3×` EXP, `<0.7×` COMP, else NORM. Win = `r_multiple > 0`. Filter pass = `samples≥10 AND win_rate≥0.55`.
- **Only 4 setups pass the filter** (the entire tradeable edge): BUY|HR_NY|ATR_EXP (73%, +0.458R), BUY|HR_NY|ATR_NORM (65%, +0.206R), SELL|HR_OVL|ATR_NORM (59%, +0.198R ← live trade), SELL|HR_LDN|ATR_NORM (59%, +0.045R weakest). Pattern: GOLD buys win in NY, sells win in LDN/OVL.
- **CRITICAL**: current build-I EA only writes `gridstat_setups.csv` (shadow DB) and `gridstat_summary.csv` (OnTester/backtest only). It **never logged live closed trades anywhere**. The on-disk `gridstat_trades.csv` is a stale backtest artifact (overwritten every optimisation) — must NOT be used as a live journal source.

**Built (Python analytics layer — analytics only, no execution):**
- `gridstat_analyser.py` — mirrors EA fingerprint+filter logic exactly; aggregates `gridstat_setups.csv` into per-setup edge table; `--classify "YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM" DIR` reports a specific entry's edge.
- `gridstat_journal.py` — durable `live_journal.json` (per-instrument: gold/silver/oil pre-wired). `sync <inst>` ingests closed live trades, tags fingerprint, records backtest expectation; `report <inst>` shows **live win% vs backtest win% drift** per setup (the learning loop: holding / watch / DECAYING). Reads live-only `gridstat_live_<symbol>.csv`, NOT the backtest trades file.

**EA change — build 2026-06-02-I → 2026-06-04-J** (live `Adapted\` folder + `ea\` backup synced):

- `AdoptOpenTickets()` in OnInit re-adopts already-open positions on recompile (regime reconstructed as `DIR|SESSION|?`, resolved later by the analyser) — so the **current open sell will still be journalled when it closes**.

Built the rigor layer, but **reframed after user pushback**: rigor SIZES bets, it does NOT add gates (see Decisions + memory rigor-drives-sizing-not-gating).
- Built `gridstat_rigor.py`: (1) **sample-uniqueness weighting** (AFML Ch.4) — duration-weighted 1/concurrency → Kish effective-N, so overlapping trend-leg signals stop inflating counts (turned out effN≈rawN here → signals mostly independent, edges are real); (2) **significance** — Wilson lower bound + t-stat on weighted R; (3) **confidence → size multiplier** (transparent, bounded 0–2×). `--write` emits `gridstat_sizing.csv` to Common\Files.
- Key result: expected-R sizing trades **9 of 15 setups** (MORE than the old win-rate≥0.55 gate's 4) — it surfaces positive-R setups the win-rate gate threw away (e.g. BUY|HR_OVL|ATR_EXP: 46% win but +0.124R; BUY|HR_NY|ATR_COMP: 93% win +0.75R). Concentrates size on 2 confirmed gold-buys-in-NY (2.0× / 1.5×). Only 6 genuinely-negative setups skipped (old filter already rejected those → zero trade-flow loss). Live SELL|HR_OVL|ATR_NORM → 0.75× = floored to broker min = still trades at 0.02.
- **EA build K**: new inputs `UseSizingTable=true`, `SizingCSVFile`, `MaxSizeMult=2.0`. `LookupSizing()` reads the sizing CSV; OnTick now sizes by expected-R table (skips mult=0 negative setups) instead of the win-rate gate.
